<meta>This page continues Kepler's philosophical inquiry into the "New Star" (the supernova of 1604). Having discussed the nature of the soul and its perception of geometry in the previous sections, he now moves toward the teleological purpose of the star: who is it for, and why did it appear during a Great Conjunction?</meta>

-> **ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-BEARER. 195** <-

By what means will Nature <note>Kepler often personifies "Natura" as a formative force or the "Soul of the World."</note> be reduced to such straits that she can do nothing but interact with us through **allegories** and **pictures**? This reasoning is challenged by the fact that we seem both to speak and to hear in our sleep; surely we conceive the phantasms of speech and words to be no less significant than other things. Furthermore, outside of man, among portents, there are even **unclear** <note>original: ἄδηλοι (*adeloi*)</note> or **absurd** voices, such as if an Ox, an embryo, or Balaam’s donkey <note>A reference to the Biblical story in Numbers 22:28, where a donkey is granted the power of speech.</note> were to speak things that can be understood. Therefore, for one who defends these views to do so correctly, it is necessary to say either that there are many such "natures"—and that those which express their meanings through voices and the likenesses of voices are rarer—or that all these modes belong to the same Creature, yet one mode is weaker and rarer than another.

Whichever of these opinions is true, this much is established on both sides: that portents, omens, dreams, and things of this sort must be explained through **allegories**.

Therefore, assuming that this star was lit by a certain design, whether of God Himself or of a rational creature, <margin>The genuine meanings of the Star overflow onto the globe of the Earth.</margin> if I am asked for what purpose I think this was done, and whether its significations are to be drawn toward those matters which we humans have at hand? First, I think not only individual nations, but even the entire globe of the Earth is too small for all our thoughts regarding the genuine meaning of a star dwelling in the highest ether to be poured into its embrace. For the vastness of the World is great; nor did the opinion of certain ancient philosophers seem absurd to **Tycho Brahe** <note>Kepler’s predecessor and the most famous observational astronomer of the era.</note>—those who established that the other globes, <margin>Whether there are inhabitants in the globes of the stars.</margin> which are most vast, also have their own inhabitants. These would not be humans, but other creatures; if they exist, they will certainly pertain to the adornment of the world, and they will not only perceive the effects of the stars, but will also be included in the providence of the Supreme Guardian. It would not be absurd if signs were sent from that highest watchtower of the sphere of the fixed stars to them just as much as to us humans living on Earth—signs perhaps more appropriately suited to their grasp than to ours. I ask, therefore, not for which nation, but rather for which **globe** <note>In this context, "globe" refers to a planet or celestial body.</note> one should believe this star was lit.

While I was wandering in this manner through the vastness of the world, I was called back again to our Earth by the **Great Conjunction** of the three planets <note>The "Great Conjunction" of Jupiter and Saturn, joined in 1604 by Mars, which occurred in the same region of the sky where the Supernova appeared.</note> which surrounded this star. For if you grant what is specific to this argument—that this meeting of the star with that conjunction was not accidental, but sought by God—you will surely be convinced that it shone most especially for the Earth; that this brilliance was assigned to the Earth and to no other globe. For nowhere except on this Earth were **Jupiter** and **Mars** seen joined on the very day that the star shone forth. Let it be, then, that the star of the year 1572 <note>The supernova observed by Tycho Brahe in Cassiopeia.</note> was lit either for Lucian’s Dog-Heads <note>Kepler refers to the *Cynocephali*, fictional inhabitants of Sirius or the Moon from the satirical works of the Greek writer Lucian of Samosata.</note> on Sirius, or for the...

Bb 2 [Catchword: Minor-]

<summary>Kepler argues that while the universe is vast and may contain other inhabitants for whom celestial signs are intended, the specific timing of the 1604 star—coinciding with a planetary conjunction visible only from Earth—proves it was a divine message specifically for humanity.</summary>
<keywords>New Star of 1604, Ophiuchus, allegories, portents, Tycho Brahe, extraterrestrial life, Great Conjunction, Jupiter, Mars, Lucian of Samosata</keywords>

--- Page 114 ---
196 JOHANN KEPLER

...about Minotaurs <note>Kepler uses the term 'Minotaurs' here metaphorically to describe monstrous or anomalous celestial sightings</note> dwelling in **Sagittarius**; because it had fallen neither on the **Ecliptic** <term>Ecliptic: the apparent path of the Sun across the sky; the plane where eclipses and most planetary movements occur</term> nor upon the joined planets: certainly this star of ours, because it stood near the Ecliptic, shone forth for the sake of the planetary spheres, as those whose motion shows us the **Zodiac**; and indeed for the sake of this Earth of ours; since from it **Jupiter** was seen to be joined to **Mars** and the new star. **Seneca** <note>Lucius Annaeus Seneca (4 BCE–65 CE), a Roman Stoic philosopher who wrote extensively on natural phenomena</note> says somewhere that nothing on this earth is so great that knowledge of it reaches that higher World. Throughout this whole book, I am in a most stubborn war against this opinion. I will answer **Seneca** from Chapter 16: to God the Creator, neither is that higher World too vast, nor are these men too small: it is enough for us that we are great enough to be the image of God the Creator.

<margin>How the allegories of portents are to be interpreted.</margin>

Therefore, hindered by nothing, I proceed to the probable Allegories which I, and others, have variously devised on the occasion of this star, and which various kinds of doctrines and philosophy produce for us. First, it seems to me thus: If Celestial portents are set before us men by the counsel of God, it is consistent that those which are plainly against the order of nature look toward those things which are supreme in human affairs; but if something is not against, but beyond the established state of nature, it looks toward some innovation in empires; finally, if something is indeed according to nature, yet somewhat more unusual or rare, it pertains to the common people, to the things born of the earth, to animals, and other such things that serve the utility of the common folk and their bodies. Nothing on earth is greater than true religion and its perpetual cultivator, the Church. Therefore, God the Best and Greatest, for the benefit of that people to whom He had given the first place in the Church, inhibited the course of the sun, changing the order of nature, and continued the space of the days by excluding the night, as a favor to **Joshua**, the sole leader of the Israelite people. At another time, when the pious King **Hezekiah** <note>A King of Judah who, according to the Bible, was granted a miracle where the shadow on a sundial moved backward as a sign of his healing</note> of that same people prayed for his safety, and God had encouraged him with the words of the Prophet **Isaiah**, He did not hesitate to confirm it by the reversal of the solar shadow on the sundial <note>original: "Scroterico," likely a variant of "Scioterico"</note>; a miracle astounding to all nations and contrary to the Nature of things. This event gained such authority for **Isaiah** as was useful to the Church in all ages for embracing those truths of faith which that Prophet had written most copiously in plainly divine words concerning the coming **Messiah**. Again, when the Head of the Church, our King and Lord **Jesus Christ**, was atoning for the sins of the human race by a shameful death: no portent in heaven or on earth, no horror could suffice, unless the light itself—which is the most horrible of all horrible things—was extinguished in a clear sky at a time when it ought not to have been.

The author of the Letter inscribed to **Dionysius the Areopagite** <note>A 1st-century judge converted by St. Paul; later writings attributed to him (Pseudo-Dionysius) were highly influential in the Middle Ages</note> to **Polycarp**, whoever he is—whether he tells a true history or imagines what could have been—attributes a speech to the philosopher **Apollophanes** that is not inconsistent with the matter he narrates.

<summary>Kepler argues that celestial events are divine messages tailored to human importance, linking the 1604 supernova to biblical miracles like Joshua's long day and the darkness at the Crucifixion.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Kepler's discussion on the astrological and providential significance of celestial phenomena, moving from historical eclipses to the interpretation of the New Star of 1604.</meta>

### ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT. 197

For when both were observing this solar eclipse together in Heliopolis, Egypt, they noted the Moon—contrary to the nature of its course—entering the disk of the sun from the east, and this at the time of a full moon <note>A natural solar eclipse can only occur during a New Moon; Kepler is referencing the miraculous darkness reported during the Crucifixion of Christ</note>. After it had stood quietly under the sun for three hours, it changed its path and proceeded back toward the east, so that the beginning of the eclipse was from the eastern edge, and the beginning of its emergence was from the western edge. Then Apollophanes exclaimed: *O noble Dionysius, these are exchanges of divine matters.* <note>original: ὦ καλὲ Διονύσιε, θείων ἀμοιβαὶ πραγμάτων</note> He compared the ordinary courses of the planets to divine matters because of the constancy of both; he did not think it possible for the Moon's course to be turned into the opposite direction unless a change in divine affairs occurred at the same time.

Nor did he construct an unfitting allegory: the Moon signifies the human race; the Sun, the Son of God; the contrary course of the Moon represents the sins of the human race; the loss of light, the passion of the Son of God. And let these be examples of the greatest events expressed by portents of this kind, which are plainly contrary to the nature of the heavens. Now Comets and **New stars**, following the category of portents—not indeed *against*, but yet *beyond* the ordinary Nature of the heavens—to what else should they be referred (among those things that are nearest to the absolute greatest of human affairs) than to empires, in which the safety of every individual man consists? Therefore, what a new Comet moving through the sky is, so on earth is some new man: one who, whether by the greatness of his empire, or by arms, or by doctrine <note>Kepler suggests that a "new man" can change the world through political power, war, or new ideas/teachings</note>, stirs up some particular fame for himself; and just as a Comet acts through terror, so this man—whether by force or some other calamity—disturbs a great part of the human race. In the year when Alexander was born, who brought the greatest slaughters to the human race by transferring the whirlwind of wars from Europe into Asia, Pliny records that there was a <term>Xiphias</term> <note>A sword-shaped comet (from the Greek 'xiphos' for sword)</note> comet. In the year both when Mithridates was born and when he took up his empire, horrible Comets shone.

If Comets moving through the sky refer to individual Monarchs, then **new stars** <margin>The New Star: of what thing it is a sign.</margin> which do not move but remain fixed in the same place, must refer to Republics or entire peoples, or to general causes pertaining to a great part of the human race.

He who enters upon this path will conclude that from this time a **New Republic** is growing to maturity, by whose general empire the kingdoms that are today variously in turmoil shall one day be subdued: so that the world, currently too restless and fierce, may for some time rest under the guardianship of this Monarchy. And if we add the conjunction of the planets, a twin definition emerges, according to how we consider them. For if it is permitted and right to conjecture the mind of God from the simple Nature of the Planets—because after three conjunctions of the mobile planets (Saturn and Jupiter, Saturn and Mars, Jupiter and Mars), which when joined are accustomed to disturb and move animal nature, a single fixed star shone forth more high and clear—it seems to be signified...

<summary>Kepler interprets the New Star of 1604 as a celestial portent of a coming "New Republic" or global peace, drawing parallels to historical comets that preceded figures like Alexander the Great.</summary>
<keywords>Supernova of 1604, astrology, comets, solar eclipse, Dionysius the Areopagite, Alexander the Great, political prophecy, planetary conjunctions</keywords>

--- Page 116 ---
198 JOHANNES KEPLER

...simply rest after the disturbances. To what does this circumstance pertain, that the star did not fall exactly upon the place of the conjunction, but a little before it? Specifically, it was so that there would not be another conjunction of the New Star with the two planets. Thus, the moment of the final conjunction had already passed; for it was at noon on the 9th of October, when on the following evening Maestlin <note>Michael Maestlin (1550–1631) was Kepler's former professor and a renowned astronomer who also observed this supernova.</note> had not yet seen the star blazing. Therefore, with this star as a guide:
*If any traces of our crime remain,*
*their removal shall free the lands from perpetual fear.* <note>original: "Si qua manent sceleris vestigia nostri / Irrita perpetua solvent formidine terras." From Virgil’s Eclogue 4, a poem often interpreted by Christians as a prophecy of the birth of Christ.</note>
Indeed, in these verses, Virgil suggests that civil unrest must be removed and the very seeds of evil rooted out. For in the consulship of Pollio, to whom Virgil writes: *And this glory of the age shall begin even with you, Pollio, as consul:* <note>original: "Teque adeo decus hoc ævi te consule inibit Pollio"</note> At the beginning of summer, Caesar and Antony, having made peace, entered Rome as friends; with what great enthusiasm from the people and with what auspicious acclamations this was done can be seen in the writings of Dio <note>Cassius Dio, a Roman historian.</note>; one of these acclamations is also this Virgilian one to Pollio.
<margin>b</margin> Nevertheless, Saturn, having been left behind before the star appeared, was joined to it within two months: as if Virgil had also said this: *Yet a few traces of the ancient deceit shall remain.* <note>original: "Pauca tamen suberunt priscæ vestigia fraudis."</note>

But if we accept this—that God had respect for astrology as it is commonly learned and practiced among men (an opinion favored by what <margin>a</margin> I said in Chapter 25: that the star seems to have been lit primarily for astronomers, because it could not be so easily noticed by the common people as that earlier star of 1572) <note>Kepler is referring to "Tycho's Star," the supernova seen in 1572.</note>—then this star would be nothing other than a kind of seal of the "Great Conjunction." It would be a confirmation of all those things which the most sober astrologers have said concerning the Great Conjunction <note>A Great Conjunction occurs when Jupiter and Saturn meet in the same spot in the sky, an event astrologers believed signaled major historical shifts.</note> based on the rules of their art, some of which we shall see below. Therefore, those things which we refuted in Chapter 30 based on "naked Nature" would here be confirmed again by the arrival of the star, through this "very great postulate." <note>original: "μέγα λίαν αἴτημα" (mega lian aitema). Kepler uses this Greek term to describe a massive leap in logic or a major assumption.</note> For nothing is incredible anymore, once a "God from the machine" <note>original: "Θεὸς ἀπὸ μηχανῆς" (Theos apo mechanes). A plot device where a deity arrives to resolve a complex situation.</note> is called upon. Thus, a period of two hundred years would be decreed, as well as the location: Hungary, Moravia, Spain—which are referred to the sign of Sagittarius. Truly, the Roman Empire, or the entire Christian Commonwealth, follows from this; for just as these are the principal empires of the whole world, so Sagittarius is at this time the principal place in the heavens, as it is the beginning of the Fire Trigon <note><term>Fire Trigon</term>: In astrology, the twelve zodiac signs are divided into four elements. The "Fire Trigon" (Aries, Leo, Sagittarius) was considered the most significant and powerful of these groupings.</note> and the place where the three superior planets <note>Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn.</note> are joined.

Fabricius <note>David Fabricius (1564–1617), a German pastor and astronomer.</note> adapts this specifically to Germany, using the argument that at the time the star could first be seen at sunset, Aries—the sign of Germany—and the equinoctial point were rising. Therefore, he attributes the entire star to a change in the Empire, and gives it a name from the Eagle, the Imperial insignia. In this matter, he seems to me to be over-subtle; for various moments can be chosen in this way, and various signs: such as Capricorn, because it was in the Midheaven at sunset; likewise...

<summary>Kepler discusses the astrological and symbolic significance of the New Star (Supernova) of 1604, linking it to Virgil's poetry and the "Great Conjunction" of planets. He debates whether the star serves as a divine "seal" confirming traditional astrological predictions about political shifts in Europe.</summary>

--- Page 117 ---
<meta>Continuing from the previous page's discussion on the astronomical positioning and astrological implications of the New Star of 1604 (Kepler’s Supernova).</meta>

->**ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-BEARER. 199**<-

Likewise, Pisces could be considered, because when the new star was <term>culminating</term> <note>reaching its highest point on the meridian</note>, Pisces was rising; likewise Gemini, because when the new star was setting, Gemini was rising. <note>original: "item Piſces, quia culminante nova piſces orti: item gemini, quia occidente nova orti gemini."</note> But it is much more appropriate to look to Sagittarius itself—not only because it provided a home to the three superior planets <note>Mars, Jupiter, and Saturn</note> and the new star, but also because when the Sun was positioned in the middle of the sky on October 10th, the very location of the new star—18 2/3 degrees of Sagittarius—rose at a polar altitude of 48 degrees. Fabricius <note>David Fabricius, a contemporary astronomer and correspondent of Kepler</note> would have emphasized this with much better justification as something worth noting. And what if we chose Libra, because when the star was rising, Libra was at its highest point and carried the Sun at its beginning? You see, therefore, that we have better reason to stay with Sagittarius, lest the astrological sign becomes uncertain. And because the star was simultaneously vertical over the Southern Ocean <note>likely referring to the Atlantic or southern latitudes</note>: one might predict from all these combined factors the union of all those parts of the world under the empire of the Spanish, or in general, of Christians.

There were in this court <note>The Imperial Court of Rudolf II in Prague</note> distinguished men who, having read my comparison of this New Star with that of the year 1572 <note>The supernova observed by Tycho Brahe in the constellation Cassiopeia</note>—noting how that one withdrew to the side of the world into an obscure and lowly corner of the heavens, while this one stood very near the *royal path* <note>the ecliptic, the path the sun and planets appear to follow</note> of all the planets—considered that in the year 1572 the Belgian provinces had newly revolted from their King. They dared to predict a good omen for the Spaniard from this modern star: namely, that the Belgians, who in 1572 had withdrawn from the King, would now return to the "royal way," that is, to the rule of their King. For some time, favorable rumors sustained these men regarding a peace between the Spanish and the Belgians, brokered by the British <note>Refers to the Treaty of London in 1604, where King James I ended the Anglo-Spanish War</note>. For, deprived of the help of neighboring Britain, and with those ports being open to the Spaniard in the future, they seemed ready to embrace safe counsels, distrusting their own strength.

The same reasoning enticed Helisaeus Röslin <note>A German physician and astrologer</note> to insist that the "Catastrophe" <note>original: "Cataſtrophen"; in this context, it means a grand turning point or resolution of a plot</note> of all European affairs, which he has been harping on for so many years, was now imminent. No argument has ever seemed so strange to me: that there was a Comet in the year 1556; then 24 years later, in the year 1580, another Comet changed its course during its duration and disappeared in Sagittarius, where after another 24 years—namely in the year 1604—the great conjunction of the Planets occurs. Therefore, he argues, in the year 1604, there will be a Catastrophe for all things begun since the year 1556.

A strange argument, I say, for even before the year 1556 there were many Comets; and between the years 1556 and 1580, there were three or four others. Therefore, the connection of that comet shining in 1556 with this one of 1580—deduced solely from an opposite sign and opposite motion—is weak. If that Providence, which shows the spectacle of Comets to men, intended for these two to be linked, why should I not rather say that the time of the "Catastrophe" of affairs conducted from the year 1556 began in the year 1580, and is clearly coming to an end in the year 1604? Indeed, the analogy would be better: those things signified by the direct Comet of 1556...

<meta>This page continues Johannes Kepler’s critical examination of the astrological predictions made by Helisaeus Roeslin concerning the Supernova of 1604 (the "New Star") and the Great Conjunction of Jupiter and Saturn.</meta>

**ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT.** 201

Now the star shines, and the Great Conjunction of the planets occurs. And as for what I wrote—that at this time there would be a "catastrophe" <note>original: *catastrophe*; here meaning a final turning point or upheaval</note> of all those affairs which began in the Christian World many years ago—this has already been partly fulfilled in England and now in Belgium; the remaining parts will shortly appear, showing where they will lead: namely, the affairs of the King of France, the Bishopric of Strasbourg <note>original: *Argentinensis*</note>, and the matters in Hungary.

Roeslin had written these things on Thursday <note>original: symbol for Jupiter ♃, used to denote Thursday</note>, October 4th, in Alsace, when behold, on the very next day, Friday <note>original: symbol for Venus ♀, used to denote Friday</note>, October 5th, the first and manifest beginnings of the Hungarian uprisings occurred. These movements, while the Christians are fighting the Turks, have remained "thorns in the eyes" until now. Meanwhile, the affairs of the Bishopric of Strasbourg ended in a turning point <note>original: καταστροφὴν</note> that was indeed milder than Roeslin had expected. And the King of France remains quiet <note>original: ἠρεμεῖ</note>; but nonetheless, he has "noted down the moon, the doctrine, and the intentions of many" <note>original: κατέγραφε καὶ τὴν σελήνην καὶ τὸ δόγμα καὶ τὰς πολλῶν διανοίας; likely a metaphorical reference to political surveillance or shifting policies</note>, and perhaps even those of Roeslin himself. "For some there is a catastrophe, but for those who were ostracized, a restoration" <note>original: Καὶ τῶν μὲν καταστροφὴ, τῶν δὲ ἐξοστρακισθέντων ἀναστροφή</note>. Thus, Roeslin wrote down almost nothing in these words that was in vain. Indeed, what he then said about the revolution of affairs in the Netherlands having begun: those things continued in the following year, 1605, and in this year, 1606, with the Spaniards seizing several fortifications on the eastern side. In England, moreover, where a catastrophe <note>original: καταστροφὴ</note> might have been hoped for or feared at that time, we have experienced a manifest restoration <note>original: ἀναστροφὴν</note> as time passed. Thus, nothing here happened according to his wish, except for those general movements of the Kingdom, which for the most part tend to toss things up and down indiscriminately, like the waves of the sea.

But let us proceed to the remaining conjectures and riddles <note>original: εἰκασμοὺς καὶ αἰνίγματα</note>. For this guesswork of Roeslin’s does not primarily follow the specific circumstances of the New Star itself, but rather pieces together signs from many different times—a practice which Tycho Brahe, in the first volume of his *Introduction to the New Astronomy* <note>original: *Progymnasmata*</note>, page 556, particularly criticized in the work of Gemma <note>Cornelius Gemma, a 16th-century physician and astronomer</note>.

There were, therefore, some who noted it as an unlucky omen that the star, having been very bright in the west, became pale and livid in the east; as if a most dismal condition were being portended for the Latin Church or the Western Empire (in the state it now flourishes) by the peoples of the East.

This circumstance could also be explained differently: namely, that the matters signified by the star would be greatest at the beginning, but would later be hidden from the minds and conversations of men (though not abolished) by the intervention of a Magistrate—who will be denoted by the Sun’s approach to the star and signified by the Sun's brilliance. Then, after some delays, these matters would emerge again, though not with the same vigor they possessed at the start. One might certainly apply the Hungarian affairs to this: with the hope of peace intervening, fears are deferred for a little while, until the peace negotiations vanish and they break out again. But then again, as at the beginning: if it is natural for a new star to gradually grow faint and finally die out, then that condition is natural; and because it was not specially sought from God, it cannot be an omen.

The history of these movements, which I described in my writing, pleased some people in a wonderful way...

Cc <note>A printer's signature mark indicating the sequence of the gathered pages</note>

### 202 THE WORKS OF JOHANNES KEPLER

...in the German text, I expressed this through riddles <note>original: "ἀινίγματα"</note>, arranged in this manner: In the Assembly <note>Kepler uses the term "Comitia," referring to a Roman political gathering</note> of Saturn, the stubborn old man, the proud Jupiter, and the warlike Mars, celebrated in the house or temple of Jupiter: Jupiter and Mars, having formed a faction and joined their votes, elected a New Star, which they would venerate with the same humility they previously showed to Saturn. Once this was done, each departed back to his own circuit. Saturn, however, is slowly girding himself to move toward the New Star; yet it will happen that even he shall submit himself to the new one—provided that she lasts—and he shall pass under her, though not much lower. This is indeed temporary; the planets depart while the New Star remains: but they shall surely return, by which time the New Star will undoubtedly be extinguished.

Nothing is said so lightly in this genre that the common people do not delight in trying to interpret it through guesswork. I believe that just as physical rest is unpleasant for children, so everyone seeks something in which to exercise their conjectures; not to find the truth, but to pass the time by reasoning and guessing. Thus, this star first received me as its interpreter, and I have found many other interpreters of my own interpretation, among them Fabricius <note>David Fabricius (1564–1617), a German astronomer and theologian who corresponded with Kepler</note>. Nor were the affairs of Hungary poorly fitted to this, if you understand the Imperial armies—going into Upper Hungary after the Turk was repelled from Esztergom <note>original: "Strigonio," a city in Hungary also known as Gran</note>—under the persona of Saturn. And yet, the motions of the heavens cannot be drawn into an omen with such subtlety. For they are necessary <term>necessary: occurring by fixed physical laws</term>; they are not arbitrary, nor are they prepared by industry. Indeed, I had warned in my German writing that I was placing a false premise at the foundation: that the star was naturally ignited by the conjunction of Jupiter and Mars.

I had also emphasized this: at the time when astronomers were expecting the three brightest stars—Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars—to be joined in a single bond <note>Kepler refers to the "Great Conjunction" of the outer planets in 1604</note>: suddenly a fourth, higher and brighter, intervened among those three. The astronomers, leaving behind those perennial planets, looked instead toward this transitory New Star. By this image, it seems signified to us—not only through the mode of nature, as said above in Chapter 28, but also from the will of the Signifier (whether God or a most powerful spirit)—that something new is to be exhibited in human affairs, in which many people will be occupied, neglecting their private business.

Indeed, it is pleasing even to predict for the dead based on their own philosophy. The dogma of Pythagoras is well known: that the souls of Heroes ascend and descend through the Milky Way. And indeed, the gate by which they begin to ascend lies open in Capricorn, while the one by which they descend is in Cancer. At this time, that opinion better supports itself, as the Milky Way stretches from Sagittarius into Capricorn; formerly it tended through Scorpio and Sagittarius. Therefore, a star in the Milky Way in Sagittarius, and specifically a northern one, will signify that some Pythagorean Hero, having completed his cycle <note>the "period" or allotted time for a soul's reincarnation</note> from this time, stands at the threshold of the lowest step, where...

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "New Star" (the Supernova of 1604) observed by Johannes Kepler, comparing its position and significance to previous celestial events in 1572 and 1600.</meta>

->**CONCERNING THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-BEARER** 203<-

where the ascent begins and the descent ends, it has even come into this lower World by making a leap. Someone might note that in the year 1572 a new star appeared in the highest part of the Milky Way in Cassiopeia <note>This refers to the famous supernova observed by Tycho Brahe</note>; then in the year 1600 another appeared a little lower in the same Milky Way and in the constellation of the Swan <note>The constellation Cygnus; this refers to the star P Cygni, which was then considered a "new" star</note>; and now this one <note>The Supernova of 1604, now known as Kepler’s Supernova</note> is in the lowest place from the Northern region of the Zodiac, very close to the Ecliptic. Therefore, this is not an ascent but a descent that has lasted 32 years.

From the ancient theology of the pagans, a blazing star ignited in the rear foot of Aesculapius <note>The Greek god of healing, identified with the constellation Ophiuchus, the Serpent-Bearer</note>—who grips the serpent with his hands and treads upon the scorpion with his foot—warns us that a most poisonous universal plague is rushing in. It is as if this star were added to the foot of Aesculapius as a spur, so that he might step forward, break through delays, and provide aid; at the same time, oil of scorpions and <term>theriac</term> <note>original: *theriaca*; a famous ancient medical paste used as a universal antidote</note> are recommended as a remedy for this plague. Furthermore, that the origin of the plague from excessive moisture is divinely sign[ified]... <note>The text breaks off here at "sign-"</note>

<summary>Kepler tracks a 32-year "descent" of new stars appearing in the sky, concluding with the 1604 supernova. He interprets the star's location in the foot of the healer constellation (Ophiuchus) as a divine omen regarding plague and the necessity of medical intervention.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Johannes Kepler’s discussion of the "New Star" (the supernova of 1604). Having compared it to the Star of Bethlehem, he now explores its potential significance for the geopolitical and religious shifts of his own era, specifically regarding the Americas and the Ottoman Empire.</meta>

204 **OF JOHANNES KEPLER**

...to seek him who, under the Star’s guidance, they believed to be born: the King of the Jews, the Savior of the world.

<margin>Whether the Indians <note>A term used in this period to refer to the indigenous peoples of the Americas.</note> are to be universally converted.</margin>
It is a great thing that in this last century a New World has been discovered by Christians, and within it have been found nations innumerable in multitude, and so savage in their barbarity that one might almost believe they were certain giants <note>Kepler is likely referencing the "Nephilim" or mythological giants, speculating on how people reached the Americas after the biblical Great Flood.</note> who caused miscarriages and fed upon human flesh, having sprung up in those regions after that universal flood.

Now we have the most certain prophecy of Christ our Savior: *This Gospel shall be preached in all the world.* We have the example of the Canaanites, to whom God first gave four hundred years in the time of Abraham, and later forty years under Moses, for repentance; and when they rendered themselves worse than before, they were utterly uprooted. Before our very eyes is a great abundance of men in Europe, yet we have implacable wars with the Turks and domestic strife among ourselves. We are driven from the East toward the West; there the Ocean receives us. We have at hand the arts and a stupendous skill for sailing, and a daily increasing desire for visiting the new "Indias." Nor are examples from antiquity lacking of how one nation has expelled another—usually the Scythians <note>Ancient nomadic people from central Eurasia, often used as a shorthand for "barbarian" invaders.</note> expelling Europeans, or more often Easterners expelling Westerners. What if, therefore, a massive <term>migration <note>original: "apoikia," a Greek term for a colony or a "home away from home."</note></term> is imminent from all Europe into those tracts of land? What if the Christian faith is to be propagated among the barbarians, and they are to be brought to civility—and for those who do not obey, as once with the Canaanites, an extreme extirpation? To what does the finger of astronomy point with the circle of the star? Or from astrology, the sign of Sagittarius and the <term>Fiery Trigon <note>An astrological event where the conjunctions of Jupiter and Saturn occur in the "fire" signs (Aries, Leo, Sagittarius). This happened in 1603-1604 and was believed to herald the rise of new empires or religions.</note></term>, which astrologers believe favors the Christian religion? So too with the "Spica" of the Virgin, with which, as Roeslin <note>Helisaeus Roeslin (1545–1616), a German physician and astrologer who also wrote on the 1604 supernova.</note> noted, the Sun stood during the rising of the star, illuminated in a sextile aspect by the star. For we assume that God uses these concepts of the astrologers to signify something to mankind.

<margin>Whether the perishing of the sect of Muhammad is signified by the star.</margin>
Prophecies of the Muslims are circulated everywhere, from which many wish to prove that this is the time in which their religion is to perish. These people will be pleased to think that God wished to indicate this very thing by the kindling of the new star in Sagittarius, which is the "triplicity" <note>A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element.</note> of the Sun and Mars, and the house of Jupiter. For the Sun and Jupiter are said by astrologers to favor Christians (whose concepts we assume God utilizes), while Mars favors the Turks. Indeed, the star agreed more with Jupiter in its region of latitude, while Mars was at its greatest Southern latitude—which on this occasion could be seen as being "depressed" or lowered. Hence, the victory of the Christian Religion over the Turkish one is concluded astrologically. If you look at the current state of Christian worship, perhaps there were never better opportunities than today; let the reader weigh the matter for himself. Is it impossible for God, out of these so many various darknesses and contentions of Christi[ans]— <note>The text cuts off mid-word "Christianorum."</note>

<summary>Kepler speculates that the supernova of 1604 and the "Fiery Trigon" portend a massive European migration to the Americas and the potential collapse of the Ottoman Empire. He suggests that God uses the "language" of astrology to communicate these historical shifts to humanity.</summary>
<keywords>Supernova of 1604, Fiery Trigon, colonization of the Americas, conversion of indigenous peoples, Ottoman Empire, Helisaeus Roeslin, astrology and providence, Sagittarius</keywords>

--- Page 123 ---
<meta>This page continues Kepler’s discussion on the astrological and providential significance of the New Star of 1604, moving from its potential meaning for the Ottoman Empire (Turks) to its significance for the Jewish people and the fulfillment of biblical prophecy.</meta>

->ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT BEARER. 205<-

...of Christianity, to bring forth some light? Or is it unbelievable that among the causes for which heresies are permitted, one is this: that the Turks, invited by these stages out of a sea of most shameful opinions, might struggle to board the ship of the Church? I know there are some steps by which some have defected from Christianity to Islam. But I wish to argue with Saint Paul. *Have they stumbled so as to fall? Far from it! But through their transgression, salvation has come to the Muslim nations.* <note>Kepler is paraphrasing Romans 11:11, substituting "the Gentiles" with "the Muslim nations" (original Latin: *gentibus Mahumetanis*) to apply the scripture to his own era.</note> Thus in the past, the Goths, first converted from Arianism <note>A theological movement that disagreed with the mainstream Church on the nature of the Trinity; many Germanic tribes were Arians before becoming Catholic.</note> to Christianity, finally fully entered the Catholic Church.

<margin>Whether the coming Messiah of the Jews is signified by the star.</margin>
It is well known that the Jews await a Messiah who is to deliver them from the bondage of the nations. To them, Saturn will be pleasing, as it agrees with the star in latitude and was joined to it almost bodily on December 11, 1604, and completed its station near it in the following August (see page 10), giving its name to the <term>Great Conjunction</term> <note>A rare alignment of Jupiter and Saturn, which astrologers believed signaled major historical shifts.</note>. Furthermore, Saturn is placed in charge of Judaism by astrologers. Therefore, although we Christians recognize Jesus, the son of Mary from the tribe of David, who was born 1,600 years ago, as the true Messiah promised by the Prophets, and thus most religiously worship Him as our Lord and God—while deploring the blindness of the Jews who, having rejected this Savior, long for another to come—nevertheless, many people (including Tycho Brahe on page 808) gather that their expectation is not entirely in vain, based on the prophecy of Saint Paul in Romans, Chapter 11, verse 25: *For I do not want you to be ignorant, brothers, of this mystery (lest you be wise in your own conceits), that blindness in part has happened to Israel, until the fullness of the Gentiles \* has entered,* <margin>\* original Greek: *eiselthē*</margin> *And so all Israel \* would be saved. As it is written: THERE SHALL COME OUT OF ZION THE ONE WHO \* SHALL DELIVER,* <margin>\* original Greek: *sōthēsetai* (shall be saved)</margin> *and shall turn away ungodliness from Jacob. And this is my covenant with them, when I shall take away their sins.* Though I do not take upon myself the interpretation of this difficult passage, I am inclined, due to other circumstances, to believe that some general conversion of the Jewish people to the faith of Christ is imminent, which one individual from among them, as an instrument of God, shall procure. For they are a most numerous people, who propagate themselves even in the midst of the greatest contempt; who, through infinite persecutions, nevertheless find a place to dwell among the nations. This is, without a doubt, by divine arrangement, for He directs the hearts of Kings as He wills. And what is most miraculous: they live by our labors while remaining idle themselves. Remove from Christian life greed, inhumanity, neglect of one’s neighbor, pride, pompous dress, and lusts, and you would fundamentally uproot the Jews from the nature of things as they currently live. <note>Kepler argues that the "social ills" of Christians provide the economic niche that allows the Jewish diaspora to persist in its current state.</note> Therefore, the matter itself cries out that this nation is even today part of divine providence. Thus it has happened that while all other nations have been confused and mixed together—to the point that very few families can trace their origin back a thousand years—the Jews alone remain, whose records are as old as the world itself. Therefore, if anyone...

Cc 3 <note>Printer's signature mark for the book's leaf.</note>

<summary>Kepler discusses the potential for the New Star to signal a mass conversion of Muslims and Jews to Christianity, arguing that the historical persistence of the Jewish people is a sign of divine providence.</summary>
<keywords>Messiah, Saturn, Great Conjunction, Romans 11, Saint Paul, Tycho Brahe, conversion, Judaism, Islam, Goths</keywords>

--- Page 124 ---
<meta>This page continues Johannes Kepler’s theological and historical reflections, specifically focusing on the conversion of non-Christian peoples and the interpretation of biblical prophecy regarding the Jews and the "fullness of the nations."</meta>

->206               JOHANNES KEPLER<-

If there is any of the interpreters who perhaps understood the cited passage of St. Paul <note>A reference to Romans 11</note> differently, he nevertheless did not wish to detract anything from future experience, being mindful of the general rule that the actual outcome of prophecies is their genuine interpreter. Let the reader compare the phrasing of St. Paul: *Blindness has in part happened to Israel, until the fullness of the nations enters in <note>original Greek: εἰσέλθῃ</note>, and so all Israel will be saved <note>original Greek: σωθήσεται</note>.* Compare, I say, these words with the phrasing of Christ in Luke 21:24: *And Jerusalem shall be trodden down by the nations <note>original: gentibus; often translated as "Gentiles"</note>, until the times of the Nations are fulfilled.*

These same probabilities lead us in the cause of the Jews, just as they did previously in the cause of the Turks. A certain learned Hebrew said to me, exclaiming loudly: *"Today you have come many thousands of miles closer to us than your ancestors did."* Truly, as they are a people with a virulent spirit and tongue, and eager for slanders, they attribute many blasphemies to Christians. Therefore, I think it should be credited to the hidden providence of God that today, with such great fervor, the opportunities for the Jews to slander are being cut away in many places. Another Jew said to me that it is common knowledge among them that for eighty years they have been treated more tolerably by the nations than in the past. And they celebrate the decree of Suleiman <note>Suleiman the Magnificent (r. 1520–1566)</note>, the Emperor of the Turks, who in the year 1540 ordered the walls of Jerusalem to be restored and water to be brought into the city through pipes.

In general, I am of the opinion that all the modern contentions and differences of worship used among Christians tend toward this: that from mutual ruin there may be an opportunity for converting the aforementioned nations—on one side the Indians <note>A 17th-century term for the indigenous peoples of the Americas</note>, and on the other the Jews and the Turks. To this point, you may not inconveniently refer to what José de Acosta <note>A Spanish Jesuit missionary and naturalist (1540–1600)</note> wrote in the 5th book of *On Providing for the Salvation of the Indians* regarding the <term>idolatry</term> <note>The worship of physical images or multiple deities, which Acosta sought to replace with Christianity</note> of the Indians and its remedies, in this manner:

Chapter 9. *“Indeed, I do not know in what words to express it: how their minds are not just imbued with, but utterly transformed into idolatry, so that they do nothing in leisure, nor in business, nor in public or private life, without first applying the superstition of their idols. They do not rejoice in weddings, nor mourn at a funeral, nor provide or receive a banquet, nor finally step foot out of the house or put a hand to work without pagan sacrilege. To such an extent the Devil holds their senses oppressed in most miserable slavery. With what great artifice they hide and disguise all these things when they see they are not free to act publicly; and again, with what great impudence they act out when they think it is permitted to them—I can only wonder at it rather than say enough about it.”* And having reviewed almost infinite types of idolatry practiced among them, he adds: *“With how many victims, how many drink-offerings, and with what a great order of ceremonies they pursue the worship of all these things...”*

<summary>Kepler discusses the potential for global conversion to Christianity, citing the rebuilding of Jerusalem's walls by Suleiman and the deep-seated religious practices of indigenous Americans as signs of a changing spiritual era.</summary>
<keywords>Johannes Kepler, St. Paul, Prophecy, Jews, Jerusalem, Suleiman the Magnificent, José de Acosta, Indigenous Americans, Idolatry, Religious Conversion</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Kepler’s discussion on the potential religious and providential significance of the 1604 supernova, drawing parallels between missionary strategies in the Americas and the potential conversion of Jews and Muslims in Europe and the East.</meta>

# ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT BEARER. [Page number] 107

...the Inca nobles, it would be an endless task to recount, etc. And by actual fact and certain experience, it has been observed that those Indian nations held more numerous and more serious superstitions of a diabolical sort in those places where the power and skill of the Kings and the Republic especially excelled, etc. Later, when in chapters 10 and 11 he <note>Referring to José de Acosta (1540–1600), a Jesuit missionary and author of *Natural and Moral History of the Indies*.</note> had taken up the remedies against idolatry to be explained, he adds this at the end: But it must be cared for with the greatest effort that, in place of harmful rites, salutary ones be introduced, and ceremonies be obliterated by ceremonies. Therefore, priests should persuade themselves that the use of holy water, images, rosaries, blessed grains, candles, palms, and the other things that the Holy Church approves and frequents, are very opportune for neophytes <note>original: "neophytis"; new converts to Christianity.</note>, and they should follow all these things with much praise in popular sermons, so that they may accustom those imbued with new and religious signs in place of the old superstitious ones. Thus it will happen that, occupied by better and more elegant things, they will lay aside the obsolete superstition of their own sect from their hands and minds. Thus says Acosta.

But if anyone should now consider the zeal of the Muslims <note>original: "Mahumetanorum"</note>, and especially of the Jews, which are diametrically opposite to these Indians: <margin>Rabbi Joseph Albo, around the year of Christ 1425, composed the book *Ikkarim*, which led many to Arianism.</margin> he will easily conclude that they will be invited by contrary remedies; and therefore, following the example of the Goths mentioned above, they could most easily of all be led over to the Antitrinitarians, then to the Arians <note>Arianism: An early Christian belief (regarded as heresy by the mainstream church) that Jesus was not co-eternal with God the Father; Kepler suggests this as a "stepping stone" for monotheistic Jews.</note>, then to the sounder assemblies of Christians—especially if they could retain the ceremonies of their own law through us, according to 1 Corinthians 7, verses 17 to 24 <note>This passage discusses remaining in the state in which one was called (e.g., circumcised or uncircumcised).</note>. To those remedies proposed for the Indians, however, they will scarcely ever arrive. 

What if, therefore, where we Europeans cannot agree among ourselves from parts so greatly in conflict, God sends some to the Indians and others to the Turks? Or perhaps even before, by a certain plan, He preserved both and placed one toward the West and the other more toward the East, so that, divided by locations, they might cease to bite and consume one another in turn; and that each, if not for itself, might at least be profitable for nations utterly foreign to the Church?

Now, as pertains to the factions of Christians among themselves: it would be a long story to bring forth from the Apocalypse of Saint John, from the Prophet Daniel, and from the enigma of the life of Christ the Savior, what each side disputes for itself: how they apply the numbers expressed in these prophecies; how some still expect the Antichrist predicted by Christ and the Apostles, while others contend he has already come. Certainly, some suspicion could arise for both from this star; for the former, of the Antichrist; for the latter, of the Judge of the living and the dead Himself being imminent. 

And because Christian poets have translated the image of the <term>Serpentarius</term> <note>original: "Serpentarii"; the constellation Ophiuchus, the "Serpent Bearer."</note> to that "Seed of the Woman" who crushes the head of the Serpent: let those whose profession this is see, therefore, how this new star, added to his back foot as a spur, can be beautifully explained by the citation of the words of Scripture, especially Apocalypse 12 and 20. It is enough to have given the warning.

Which...

<meta>This page continues Johannes Kepler’s discussion from his 1606 work "De Stella Nova" (On the New Star), where he investigates the nature of the supernova observed in 1604 and its potential astrological and theological significance.</meta>

# 208 JOHANNES KEPLER

Therefore, as it pertains to that salvific arrival of our Lord Jesus Christ—most longed-for and most certain to all true Christians—for the Judgment and the <term>consummation of the age</term> <note>original Latin: "consummationem seculi," a phrase referring to the end of the world or the completion of the current era of human history.</note>: just as some have said that the returning <term>Fiery Trigon</term> <note>An astronomical phenomenon where the conjunctions of Jupiter and Saturn occur in the "fire" signs of the zodiac (Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius) over a 200-year period. Kepler believed these cycles marked major historical shifts.</note> is its sign, as was stated above; so there will be no shortage of those who would make this star <note>This refers to the Supernova of 1604, which appeared near a significant conjunction of planets.</note> the harbinger of that same event. They are encouraged in this by that Star of the Magi which had preceded the first coming of Christ. 

I certainly find less fault with the religious devotion of these people than I do with the curiosity of those earlier men, who attempted to adapt a matter that is—beyond any natural controversy—part of the natural order, to this supernatural work. For why should I rebuke those who imitate Christ the Lord? He commanded his followers to keep watch because they do not know at what hour he will come; and they are those who, at every celestial and earthly sign which [humanity]...

--- Page 127 ---
<meta>This page continues Johannes Kepler's astrological and physical analysis of the "New Star" (the Supernova of 1604) appearing in the constellation Ophiuchus (the Serpent-Bearer).</meta>

->ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT. 209<-

...signifies changes among the people, and perhaps even a **New Sect**, as other astrologers have suggested. Because the star preceded those planets in both time and position—in terms of both longitude and latitude—it therefore promises peace following these quarrels. Furthermore, because the star stood near the <term>royal path<note>The ecliptic, the apparent path of the Sun across the celestial sphere, which the planets also follow.</note></term> of the planets, it hints at the end of confusion and deviation, and the restoration of **Order**. Some will interpret this as a return to the Catholic church, while others will see it as a return to the simplicity of Apostolic worship.

And because this was the beginning of the <term>Fiery Trigon<note>A significant astrological cycle where the "Great Conjunctions" of Jupiter and Saturn occur in the fire signs: Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius. These were historically believed to herald world-altering events every 800 years.</note></term>, which has been distinguished by the most important turning points in history—such as the Creation of the World and the Birth of Christ <note>original: "Nativitate Christi". See chapter 7, and Tycho Brahe in the conclusion of his *Introductory Exercises* (Progymnasmata), page 805.</note>—it therefore signifies a certain calling back to the original principles of Christianity. 

Finally, because the star was higher <note>In the sense of celestial distance or dignity</note> than Jupiter and Mars, yet appeared alongside them—Jupiter signifying pomp, pride, and authority, while Mars signifies restlessness, battles, quarrels, seditions, the fury of the common people, and rash impulses—therefore there is signified...

--- Page 128 ---
<meta>This page continues Kepler’s astrological and theological interpretation of the "New Star" (the supernova of 1604). In the previous sections, he discussed the physical properties and the timing of the star's appearance. Here, he pivots to the potential meaning of its proximity to Saturn and the grim political omens he perceives.</meta>

->210 JOHANNES KEPLER<-

Although as far as this determination of timing is concerned, we have overstepped the bounds of our own principles. For if the place and time of the star at 18 degrees of Sagittarius were chosen by God because of the gathering of Jupiter and Mars, then that meeting of Saturn with the New Star after the sixtieth day was a natural necessity, not something specifically sought by God. Therefore, either God arranged the entire significance of the matter according to a foreseen specific pattern of celestial motions—which is absurd (<note>original: "nihil enim hominibus mittit propter coelum; sed ad ea quae missurus est hominibus, significanda, ponitur uti coelo."</note> for He sends nothing to humanity for the sake of the heavens; rather, He uses the heavens to signal those things He intends to send to humanity)—or, in the matter which God signaled through the star’s conjunction with Jupiter and Mars, Saturn’s meeting with the New Star could naturally produce those effects we mentioned regarding the sixtieth year. This, again, is most absurd: to decide on a matter divinely presented as being outside the mode of nature. Or, because neither of these is true, we will not be able to use this meeting of Saturn with the New Star to form our conjectures.

It is, however, not difficult to gather this: because the star lasted more than a full year, it signifies something very long-lasting; for no comet has ever endured so long.

Although I do not predict these things concerning a change for the better with as much confidence as Roeslin <note>Helisaeus Roeslin (1545–1616), a physician and astrologer who, unlike Kepler, was prone to bold, apocalyptic predictions.</note> has used. For what if that "Saturnian religion," or that "Saturnian Prophet," who is signified by the New Star and its agreement in latitude <note>In astronomy, "latitude" refers to the distance of a celestial body north or south of the ecliptic plane.</note> with Saturn, is to be interpreted far differently? My spirit shudders <margin>When the Son of Man comes, do you think he will find faith on the earth? <note>A reference to Luke 18:8.</note></margin> to explain what omens the present face of affairs and the examples met with across all nations supply to me. But <term>Harpocrates</term> <note>The Greek god of silence and secrecy; Kepler uses this to indicate he is choosing to remain quiet about certain dangerous political or religious conclusions.</note> conveniently intervenes. Truly, Saturn is cold, Saturn is a scorner, Saturn turns everything upside down. Saturn, under pressure and constraint, feigns and hides everything, investigates everything, scrutinizes everything, suspects everything, makes everything suspicious, hardens the heart, and puts on stubbornness. He is the only one who could unite even the Turks with wicked Christians in a single alliance—though a secret and unacknowledged one. Nor is any punishment so great that we have not deserved it.

<margin>Epilogue.</margin>
I have set forth all the ways by which I think one can arrive at the meaning of the star through some methodical reasoning. I ask the reader, again and again, to judge these based on the principles I have assumed. For if you deny what I have assumed in each place, the things I have built upon them will also collapse. That one thing I have often repeated above, I now emphasize again: very little room is left for the "subtlety of disputation" (which many will think I have used) in this business where, as experience witnesses, "Enthusiasms" <note>Kepler refers to divine inspiration or intuitive insight (*enthusiasmos*) rather than just cold logic.</note> carry such weight. Since the whole matter is divine, the interpretation of the matter is also divine, as was said above.

Moreover, I ask those readers who know me more closely not to turn it into a fault if they find my theological and political discourses to be shorter than they perhaps expected...

<summary>Kepler expresses skepticism toward overly specific astrological predictions, arguing that Saturn's influence suggests a period of secrecy, stubbornness, and religious/political decay rather than an easy "change for the better." He concludes by emphasizing that interpreting such divine signs requires spiritual intuition rather than just technical logic.</summary>

<keywords>Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, New Star of 1604, Helisaeus Roeslin, Harpocrates, astrology and theology, political omens, divine signs</keywords>

--- Page 129 ---
<meta>This page continues Kepler’s discussion on the astrological and providential meaning of the New Star of 1604 (Nova Serpentarii), specifically addressing his role as Imperial Mathematician and his refusal to provide specific political prophecies.</meta>

->ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-HOLDER [space] 211<-

I have not included anything concerning the conflicts of these times or their catastrophes; let people instead consider my position. I was hired by the **Emperor** <note>Rudolf II, the Holy Roman Emperor and Kepler's patron.</note>, not to be a public prophet, but to complete—to the best of my abilities—the science of astronomy, which was left deprived of its true Master, **Tycho** <note>Tycho Brahe (1546–1601), the preeminent astronomer whose data Kepler inherited.</note>. In this duty, I must take account of all people for whom the Great Emperor strives to earn favor through this refined and perfected Astronomy. I should not write such trivial side-projects for the sake of private interests, for others might be deterred or alienated by them, and perhaps the **main task** <note>original: "τὸ ἔργον"</note> itself which I am handling might be neglected by everyone. I consider this restriction to be a very great benefit. I am certain that if I had been free to express the feelings of my mind in print, and to step outside the limits of my profession into Politics and the state of the Church (because of how these things are related), there are no warring parties today that I would not have offended with my writing. Truly, *sin is committed both inside and outside the walls of Troy.* <note>A quote from the Roman poet Horace, meaning that faults and errors are found on both sides of a conflict.</note>

Nor was I without fear that the same thing would happen to me as happened to others—such as to **Fabricius**, <note>David Fabricius, a contemporary astronomer and pastor who also observed the New Star.</note> whose writing on the significations of this star contains nothing other than complaints about his neighbors, opinions on the state of the Empire, and a desire for vengeance and reform. Thus he shapes his oracles as if he were an orator hired by the Magistrate under whom he lives—partly to terrify rebels and evil neighbors, and partly to win over and rouse the Emperor. In the same way, **Crabbus** <note>likely Paul Krabbe, another contemporary astronomical writer.</note> plainly acts the part of a counselor, showing that Christian affairs are perishing through sluggishness and sleepiness, and that the **Turkish enemy** <note>A reference to the Ottoman Empire, then a major military threat to the Holy Roman Empire.</note> grows stronger by this means. He does not consider that whatever he relates, whether true or false, he relates in vain; for these things should not have been proposed in a prophetic forecast, but in a secret Council—or else he should have remained silent. The heavens tell us no such thing. Indeed, the indicators of diseases are always more numerous than the physicians. By such things, these men expose themselves to the ridicule of many.

Therefore, both my own condition and God Himself, by His own example, have provided me with the most wholesome advice. He displayed a general sign to the eyes of all parties to be looked upon, so that He might warn individuals to look into themselves and examine their own vices. Having recognized their errors and their crimes, they might be converted to true repentance. **Tycho Brahe** also followed this same path for the most part; there is no reason why I should not follow closely in his footsteps.

Doubtless, the vanity of the common people is great, and their habit is most perverse: when some omen is shown by divine power, they immediately turn their eyes and ears away from it toward the interpreter, regarding the interpreter himself as the new omen. They are concerned not with what reasons he uses, but entirely with what he says will happen—which is the truly "prophetic" part to them. They believe that for the sake of a single petty astrologer, and to recommend his hasty opinions, a star was lit by God in the heavens. I do not attribute these things to the common people out of malice or exaggeration; the thing I describe is the reality. Consider, I ask you, anyone who is a little more religious, a little more inclined toward humanity...

<margin>
Dd 2
</margin>

He criticizes other astronomers for using the New Star to push personal or political agendas, arguing that the star is a universal call to repentance rather than a specific political omen.</summary>

--- Page 130 ---
<meta>This page concludes the main text of Kepler's 1606 work on the New Star (the 1604 supernova). Kepler distinguishes his scientific astronomical work from the popular, often superstitious demand for astrological prophecy.</meta>

212 **JOHANNES KEPLER ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT** <note>original: "IOAN. KEP. DE STELLA NOVA SERP." The "Serpent" refers to Ophiuchus, the Serpent Bearer, where the supernova appeared.</note>

...more inclined toward [divine] will, he is not attentive to every word of the astrologer as if they were so many oracles: thinking entirely that all things are said and done by the hidden disposition of God; however much he understands the flaws in the arguments which the astrologer uses. For people of this kind, no explanation of a matter ever suffices that stays within the boundaries of the art; rather, they demand from the astrologer that he place the most intimate feelings of the soul—through the circumstances of places, times, persons, religions, and public affairs—most nakedly before the eyes of men. Unless the astrologer obeys them—that is, unless he becomes the most foolish of mortals—he then incurs various rebukes: they deny that he acts candidly, they accuse him of timidity, flattery, desertion, and a thousand crimes; nor do they distinguish between astronomy <note>the mathematical study of stars</note> and astrology <note>the practice of predicting human events from stars</note>; thinking there will be no utility for astronomy unless it is so confused with these external circumstances involved in the public custom of life through the divining art, that all of life, even the slightest actions of men, are tied to the heavens and predefined to happen. To these people, therefore, I wish this to be said in place of a conclusion: let them go away with their curiosity to the prophecies of Abbot Joachim, Saint Bridget, Merlin, and the rest; and to the predictions of Antonius Torquatus, Carion, Paracelsus <note>original: "Theophrasti"</note>, Jacob Hartmann, Misocacus, Sibald Brandinus, and Herlicius <note>These were all popular figures known for making prophetic or astrological claims</note>, who "set their mouth in the heavens" <note>A biblical reference to Psalm 73:9, implying they speak with arrogant or divine authority</note> and recognize no judge except the Outcome; and finally to those who have treated these men according to their merits, such as Johannes Rasch, Paul Messing, and others, and let them delight themselves there. But from this little book, which I have dedicated entirely to astronomical and natural speculations, let them keep their eyes—which are most hostile to this subject matter—away.

To the others, who are the opposite of these and praise moderation, I hope I have proven myself a good and peaceful German: who, if they note anything here that seems to be too much, will attribute it to the astrologers whose more general predictions had to be examined; if, however, they are to find some things in which I have not satisfied them, let them consider that this is the condition of mortality. Indeed:

*It has always been permitted for good men to feel differently about the same things,*
*While keeping their friendship unharmed.* <note>original: "Non eadem sentire bonos de rebus ijsdem / Incolumi licuit semper amicitia."</note>

Nor can anyone be so quick to criticize that I would not surpass them in the ease of my compliance when they advise me rightly.

->**T H E   E N D .**<-

--- Page 135 ---
<meta>This page continues Kepler's defense of the Copernican system in the context of the "New Star" of 1604. Having discussed the star's position, he now addresses the physical implications of the Copernican model, specifically the massive distances required between the planets and the stars.</meta>

->ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT BEARER. 83<-

On the immensity of the Sphere of the fixed stars in the hypotheses of Copernicus: and on the size of the New star.
### CHAPTER XVI.

**A**t this point, I am certain that some of those who read this will laugh at the "insanity" of Copernicus; others, who embrace his opinion due to other astronomical and physical arguments, will nonetheless be offended by this immensity. They will begin to doubt whether the arguments brought from other sciences to stabilize the opinion of Copernicus are truly stated, or whether there might be some way to dissolve those arguments and show how the Copernicans were led into error. 

<margin>What Brahe was accustomed to object against Copernicus.</margin> And Brahe <note>Tycho Brahe (1546–1601), the great Danish astronomer whose data Kepler used. Brahe rejected the Copernican system partly because it required a massive "empty" space between Saturn and the stars.</note> added strength to this objection, as he sought a sense of proportion in a most perfect work <note>i.e., the Universe created by God.</note>; he argued it would be absurd if the vastness of the single sphere of fixed stars were so "insane," while the size of all the moving planets was so contemptibly small. Just as, he says, it would be a huge defect in the human body if a finger or a nose were many times larger than the mass of the entire rest of the body. Therefore, to dilute this objection as the occasion arises, I will say three things: first, that much more incredible things result from the opinion of the ancients and Ptolemy regarding the motion of the fixed stars; second, I will establish the inestimable magnitude of this proportion using other examples taken from the world; finally, I will show a mutual compensation by which a certain equality arises out of these most unequal things.

<margin>What the proportion of motions is according to Copernicus.</margin> Therefore, as pertains to the motion of the worldly bodies, in the Copernican hypotheses <note>Kepler uses "hypotheses" here to mean the mathematical and physical model of the universe.</note> the globe of Saturn in one hour traverses nearly 300 German miles <term>German miles (milliaria germanica)<gloss>A unit of distance used by Kepler, roughly equal to 7.5 kilometers or 4.6 miles.</gloss></term>, Jupiter 400, Mars 600, the center of the Earth 740, Venus 800, and Mercury 1000. This is a beautiful proportion, where the motion is always swifter for the body that is closer to the resting Sun, the dispenser of all motion. And because the Earth is not only moved annually but also revolves daily: in one hour, the parts of the Earth lying under the equator travel 240 German miles apart from the center's movement, while the other parts travel less, as each is nearer to the poles. Therefore, by adding the 240 miles of the surface to the 740 of the center, nearly one thousand miles result—this is the amount of ethereal space a city situated under the Equator measures out in one hour at midnight. Likewise, by subtracting 240 from 740, there remain 500 miles, the journey of one hour at noon, under the Equator, through the ethereal air. From this, one must consider that since the crossing is twice as fast... 

<summary>Kepler defends the Copernican model's vast distances by arguing that the resulting speeds of the planets create a "beautiful proportion" where closer planets move faster. He begins to refute Tycho Brahe's objection that a giant gap between planets and stars would be "unnatural" or "insane."</summary>
<keywords>Copernicus, Tycho Brahe, planetary motion, German miles, celestial proportions, speed of Earth, heliocentrism, sphere of fixed stars</keywords>

84 JOHANNES KEPLER

<margin>On the cause of the coldness of the nights and the morning time.</margin>
...the passage of the earth, being different by night than by day under the Equator; whether this might contribute something to the coldness of nights, as if by a kind of ventilation; and whether, for this same reason, the morning time feels more refreshing. This would be because the parts of the earth where the Sun is just rising are, at that moment, carried directly into the <term>ethereal breeze</term> <note>Kepler imagines the "ether" filling space acts like a very thin fluid or wind that the Earth moves through.</note>; whereas the parts where the Sun is setting are, at that time, essentially being withdrawn from that ethereal breeze. We are left with the Moon, which completes 476 miles in one hour relative to the center of the Earth, because it is 60 <term>semidiameters</term> <note>A semidiameter is the radius of the Earth.</note> distant from the Earth. Add 476 to the 740-mile journey of the Earth’s center, and the journey of the Full Moon rises to 1216 miles per hour through the ethereal breeze. Subtract, in turn, 476 from the center’s journey of 740, and there remains the journey of the New Moon: 264 miles in one hour. You have here the proportionate speed of all the stars according to Copernicus.

<margin>The stillness <note>original Greek: *erēmesis*</note> of the fixed stars.</margin>
For the fixed stars stay in their place and remain at rest. The Sun acts as the Motor and maintains an immobile station in the middle of the World—except that I have established it as consistent with physical reasoning, in my commentaries on the motions of the star Mars <note>This refers to his famous 1609 work, *Astronomia Nova*.</note>, 

<margin>The rotation <note>original Greek: *dinēsis*</note> of the Sun.</margin>
that the Sun's body rotates within its own space. By this rotation, it drives an <term>immaterial species</term> <note>An "immaterial species" is Kepler's term for a physical force, similar to magnetism, that emanates from the Sun to push the planets.</note>, a motive force, throughout the vastness of the World. All the wandering stars [planets] follow this force: the nearer each one is, the faster it moves.
Lest this passage of a thousand miles in a single hour still seems incredible to you, I bid you to consider the ratio of the density of air to the density of the ether, which I demonstrated in my *Optics*. From this it follows that a passage of a thousand miles in one hour through the ether is many times—an incredible number of times—more tranquil than a passage of a single mile in one hour through our air.

<margin>The incredible swiftness of motions in Ptolemy and the accepted opinion.</margin>
Go now to Ptolemy and the ancient opinion; you will find everything there to be even more incredible. In that system, the <term>semidiameter</term> of the sphere of the fixed stars is twenty thousand times the Earth's semidiameter; therefore, the circumference will be sixty-three thousand. This is a modest number indeed compared to the Copernican distances, but it is said that all of it must revolve in a single day! Therefore, 2,625 semidiameters are owed to a single hour, each of which contains 860 miles <note>Kepler is likely using "German miles," which are much longer than modern miles—about 4.6 modern miles each.</note>. 
Observe here the immense difference: Saturn, which in Ptolemy's system is nearest to the fixed stars (so close it nearly touches them), travels 300 miles in one hour according to Copernicus. According to Ptolemy, it travels two million, two hundred fifty-seven thousand, five hundred miles. Thus, Saturn must be believed to be faster in Ptolemy’s system than in Copernicus’s by seven thousand five hundred twenty-five times. Whoever attempts to grasp this incredible velocity in their mind will be just as exhausted—and even more violently so—than one who contemplates the Copernican vastness.

<summary>Kepler calculates the hourly speeds of the Earth and Moon, arguing that the sun-centered model is more physically plausible than the Earth-centered model, which requires the outer planets and stars to move at impossible speeds.</summary>
