176 JOHANNES KEPLER

...the story of the first chapter. By this name <note>original: "Hoc nomine"</note>, the star signified various rumors, various omens of future events, the consternation and amazement of the common people, many hindrances to many businesses, and many new occupations. As to what happened to individuals, let them say so themselves; as for me, I have recounted it in the preface. 

I had to look toward **Saturn, Jupiter, Mars, Venus, and Mercury**; but having set those aside and delayed the labor of investigating the perpetual motions from the observations of **Tycho Brahe** <note>Kepler worked as the assistant to the Danish astronomer Tycho Brahe and inherited his massive collection of precise planetary data</note>, I turned my eyes to the New Star, and I remained speechless before it. You may ask: what did the new star portend? Certainly many writings by many people about the new star, and many occupations for printers, who were driven by both profit and the awareness of human curiosity. 

Whether there were fewer of these writings than I portended in my own little pages, written in the native tongue <note>original: "vernaculo idiomate"; Kepler is referring to his German-language pamphlet "Bericht vom neuen Stern" published in 1604</note>, I do not know. I do know that I did my part to ensure that my own prophecy would not be fulfilled by my own pages, for I printed no more than two hundred copies and added the prohibition: **LET NO ONE IMITATE THIS** <note>This was a standard copyright warning against pirated editions</note>. Yet, another man sitting in Amberg returned this voice—just as an Echo is wont to do—exactly as he received it. 

<margin>The Echo of Amberg.</margin>

**THE AMBERGIANS HAVE IMITATED IT.** Indeed, both they and the people of **Strasbourg** <note>original: "Argentinenses"</note> have imitated it. Therefore, I am grateful to them, for they fulfilled my prophecy through my own little work, yet without my own effort. 

Is there indeed anything more that this star portended in my own studies? Why not? Indeed, I would not have made a description of that part of the sky unless a new Star had arisen in it; I would not have devised so many things about the **secrets of nature** in this very book, which I am writing and publishing here, unless this new Star had come forth from Nature’s secrets. Therefore, if this "new philosophy" of mine seems absurd to certain Philosophers: let them consider it a "portent" of a commentary, for the sake of which they can say that portentous Star was lit—so that, perhaps, the star might not seem to have been in vain for them.

The star was able to do none of these things by itself; it sent down nothing to the earth except its own brightness. But when its image was received into the eyes of men, and recognized as new by astronomers (who had acquired this science through practice, exercise, and study), and when those men were either placed in such positions that they were incited to write by reason of their duty, or they practiced the art of printing and lived by it and knew of human curiosity—these causes, I say, concurring on the earth, those effects are then linked to the New Star. 

In this way, the star provides nothing but the **occasion** <term>Occasion: In philosophy, an "occasional cause" is not the direct force that makes something happen, but the circumstance that triggers a response from a different agent</term>. The "sovereignty" of the entire cause lies within the perception of the star and within the men themselves. You will find that this fits beautifully both with many things said above and with those that will follow.

In the same way, if there could be any other perception or estimation of the star—namely an astrological or a common one—that could lead men to various other actions.

<summary>Kepler humorously argues that the "prophecy" of the New Star was fulfilled by the flurry of books written about it, including pirated editions of his own work. He provides a philosophical argument that stars do not "cause" earthly events directly; rather, they provide the light/occasion that triggers human reactions and interpretations.</summary>

to induce. For what if the common crowd of men, raised in expectation of new things by the celebration of the New Star, more easily admitted something new; persuaded that it was fated, and promoted by a certain necessity, since even a prodigious star had been lit for its sake? What if certain private individuals throughout Hungary and Britain, led by astrological reasoning, interpreted this star as a good omen (for many praised it for the most pleasant brightness of its light), and because it had shone in the house of Jupiter, they judged it to patronize the true religion (which both sides estimated from their own judgment or that of their party), and thus dared to attempt those things of which all posterity will speak? What if there existed someone who meditated on articles of a new religion or a new Peace in religion, confirmed by the consideration of this star—not astrologically, but in a simple and common way—because it stood above the conjoined Planets. If such a person hides anywhere, who either arrogates to himself the office of prophecy or is conscious of power to fulfill his destinies: I would indeed think him prefigured by that monster which was born this year 1606, on the 2nd/12th day of January, at Strasbourg <note>original: Argentinæ</note>. <margin>Monster</margin> Two little girls, whose lower bellies were separate, with two livers, each with its own veins directed to its own arms <margin>skilfully</margin> and feet; for the sake of which there was a double spine and <margin>made.</margin> a double cerebellum in a doubled occiput <note>The back part of the skull</note>, so that the motor nerves could go into the doubled arms and feet; likewise, there were two pairs of ears, because the auditory nerves arise from the cerebellum, which here was double. These two little girls, I say, joined above the navel facing each other into one chest, but with their heads united behind a single face; they received one pair of eyes, one nose, one mouth, and therefore one throat, one lung, one heart, likewise one gullet, and one stomach. 

And if indeed the father of the monster is said to have been a joiner <term>arcularius: a maker of chests or cabinets</term>, whose daily work in that craft is to glue together <margin>Cause of the</margin> well-hewn and smoothed boards; so that a suspicion arises of an <margin>monster.</margin> "imaginative impression" made on the fetus of the pregnant wife <note>Kepler refers to the contemporary belief that a mother's strong thoughts or sights could physically shape her unborn child</note>: nevertheless, that Admirable Artisan <note>referring to God or Nature</note> who glued these little girls together applied an incredibly greater wisdom—as anatomists will admit—than joiners usually do, having found a way by which two might live from the same principle. 

Indeed, the world has long been sated with contentions regarding matters of faith; yet hope is not near that, while the remaining parts have died away, one alone will prevail in the world. For what if it happens to those factions that fight today as it did with the ancient sects of Christians, that they endure for a very long time in the world: just as alongside the Latins <note>The Roman Catholic Church</note>, there endured through Asia and Africa the Greeks, Judaizing Syrians, Eutychian Jacobites, and Nestorian Tatars—of whom the former possessed vast kingdoms through Africa, and the latter through Northern Asia; besides the Monothelite Maronites, Armenians, and Georgians. And so, whoever strives to find a way by which two thinking differently...

<meta>This page continues the author's astrological and physical analysis of the "New Star" of 1604 (Kepler's Supernova). He is moving from technical observation to the "significatio" or the potential influence of the star on human affairs and history.</meta>

-># ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT. 179<-

Indeed, those who judge these matters as too small to recognize in them a hidden <margin>There are great disturbances today.</margin> stirring of human passion—one not undertaken by choice due to obvious and familiar causes, but flowing from some internal bitterness of nature—I think the same thing happens to them as to those walking in a fog. As long as they see only each other, they do not realize the thickness of the mist surrounding them; but when they view the fog from a distance, standing in the clear sunlight, only then do they marvel at the density that was clouding their eyes. Thus, let those who wish downplay today’s turmoils; it is certain that once some time has passed and all that is happening today has been recorded, readers will judge these times to have been altogether more tumultuous than others.

In the year 1572, after that famous star in Cassiopeia <note>original: "Cassiopejæ stella" — referring to the supernova observed by Tycho Brahe in 1572.</note> shone forth, the provinces were for the most part quiet compared to others. But in <margin>What followed the star in the year 1572.</margin> the Netherlands and France, where affairs were already disturbed around that time, such a great renewed commotion followed from that year onward—not with a single blow, but through a long-weaving thread—that entire illustrious families and entire Republics were utterly overthrown, and new ones arose. There is still no end to these turmoils, even though very few of those who were present at the first beginnings now remain; both sides have been most miserably punished, including those who prudently withdrew in flight and settled in other regions, only to be wretchedly harassed by the envy of the locals and by poverty.

By introducing this one example, whether I argue well or fruitlessly, I certainly propose this one thing without harm: I believe that the rulers of Republics, and even Monarchs themselves, ought to keep their eyes and minds fixed on this example and provide diligently so that today’s widespread bitterness among men does not gain further ground. They must ensure that even now, the seeds of long-lasting calamities are not being sown within Germany as well as in neighboring lands, and that where such seeds appear to have been sown, they do not eventually grow and take deep root through negligence or the refusal of even the smallest effort or inconvenience. If they do, they may later become impossible to uproot without the destruction of the entire field.

To finally conclude what I believe can be said according to the order of nature regarding the new star: this too must be considered, that since <margin>The effect of the new star on those born under it.</margin> this star stood very close to the <term>Ecliptic<gloss>The apparent path of the Sun across the celestial sphere; stars near this line were thought to have greater influence on Earth.</gloss></term>, joined with three planets, and possessed a celestial light similar to the fixed stars, it is consistent with reason that its likeness—just like that of the other Planets and fixed stars—is received into the natures of humans being born. Therefore, those who were born around the 9th and 10th of December in the <term>New Style<gloss>The Gregorian Calendar, which had been adopted by Catholic states but was still being debated in Protestant regions at this time.</gloss></term> had the Sun joined to the new star; to say nothing of those who, during the entire time of its duration, came into the world during the hours when the star was <term>culminating<gloss>Reaching its highest point in the sky.</gloss></term> or rising. And it is consistent to think that all these people have been granted a nature <term>tending toward revolution<gloss>original Greek: νεωτερίζουσαν (neoterizousan) — meaning "innovating" or "restless for change," often used in a political or rebellious sense.</gloss></term>. It is not advisable for them to pass down today’s disturbances as if by hand, only to be mixed up in them themselves when they have grown up. Great is the power of imagination in pregnant women...

<summary>Kepler warns that the 1604 supernova portends significant political unrest, drawing a parallel to the upheaval following the 1572 supernova. He suggests that children born while the star was visible may inherit a restless or "revolutionary" temperament.</summary>

ON THE NEW STAR IN THE SERPENT-HOLDER. 181

# On the natural effects of the Fiery Trigon.

## CHAPTER XXIX.

**[Decorative initial]** This separate explanation must be inserted here, not only because the description of the <term>Fiery Trigon<note>The "Fiery Trigon" refers to a period of roughly 200 years during which the "Great Conjunctions" of Jupiter and Saturn occur in the three zodiac signs associated with the element of fire: Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius. Astrologers of the era believed these cycles marked major historical shifts.</note></term> would seem incomplete unless a few answers were given to the infinite predictions of astrologers drawn from it, but also because in the following chapters, the circumstances of the Fiery Trigon will be intertwined with the study of the new star. This is done so that it may clearly appear in what respect this happens; what the Fiery Trigon can achieve by itself, and what part is aided by the star. Finally, it is done to separate those things which, according to the decrees of astrologers, are attributed to the "Great Conjunction," from those things which various prejudices and hopes or fears have suggested to astrologers—and specifically to my friend **Fabricius** <note>David Fabricius (1564–1617) was a German pastor and astronomer who corresponded frequently with Kepler and was one of the first to observe the new star.</note>. For according to the principles used in Chapters 26 and 27, it can be believed that God Himself has some regard for those things which are drawn with a sincere mind from astrological precepts; thus, it is most profitable to have those precepts thoroughly cleansed of error. And indeed, it has been stated in the most recent chapters, and also scattered throughout the earlier ones, what this conjunction of Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars (which began the Fiery Trigon in the years 1603 and 1604) was naturally able to do to the "formative faculty" of the terrestrial globe—the power that governs <term>Meteors</term> <note>In Kepler’s time, "meteors" referred to any atmospheric phenomena, including rain, wind, snow, and lightning, not just "shooting stars."</note>.

The decrees of the astrologers are certainly framed in such a way that they could strike fear of a world-wide conflagration into a melancholic man. Certain hot spells in the following years were already beginning to confirm their persuasion; yet this very summer of the year 1606, which has exceeded all measure in its dampness, has utterly uprooted that belief once again. Thus it was in the year 1524, when astrologers, based on their opinion of a "Watery Trigon," predicted mournful and horrific floods: some gullible people abandoned the plains and retreated to the mountains. And yet, they did not perish by water; rather, not long after, many perished in the flooding blood of the Peasants’ War <note>The German Peasants' War (1524–1525) was a massive popular revolt. Kepler is using this historical irony to show that astrological labels like "Watery" or "Fiery" do not literally dictate the weather.</note>. Therefore, the expectation of drought and scorched earth based solely on the name of the "Fiery Trigon" is vain. <margin>The effect of the Fiery Trigon on Meteors.</margin> For there will be just as much rain, snow, and flood during these two hundred years as there were fires, lightning bolts, and droughts during the completed Watery Trigon. For it was said in Chapter 6 that the designations from the "Elements"—and thus also the name "Fiery"—came to these Trigons not from their own nature, much less from their actual effects, but from the mere whim of astrologers. Oh, the infinite vanity of astrologers, who never seem to learn, and never cease to build their prognostications upon these most futile foundations of playful naming!

What? Have not some progressed to such a point of madness that, because we Christians hold it as certain that the Heavens and the Earth which now exist are reserved for Fire on the Day of Judgment, <margin>The Fiery Trigon is not...</margin> they believe this...

<summary>Kepler argues that the "Fiery Trigon" is a human-made label rather than a physical cause of weather, debunking astrologers' fears of a world-ending fire by citing the rainy summer of 1606 and the failed flood predictions of 1524.</summary>
<keywords>Fiery Trigon, Great Conjunction, astrology, weather, 1606 summer, 1524 flood, David Fabricius, Peasants' War, meteors, terrestrial faculty</keywords>

<meta>The text on this page continues a discussion from the previous page regarding the "Fiery Trigon," likely concluding a word such as "reasoning" or "observation" (Latin: rationem/observationem) regarding the comparison of history and astronomy.</meta>

...the method of comparing histories and the eras of religions with Astronomy. Some of these are presented for inspection in the table in the seventh chapter. Nevertheless, anyone who diligently weighs all the evidence will realize that a new state of affairs did not suddenly emerge precisely at the moments when the <term>Fiery Trigon<note>An astrological period of approximately 200 years during which the "Great Conjunctions" of Jupiter and Saturn occur in the fire signs: Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius. These were historically viewed as precursors to major historical shifts.</note></term> returned. Indeed, whatever experience can naturally attribute to these shifts of the Trigons—or rather, to the <term>Great Conjunctions<note>The meeting of the planets Jupiter and Saturn in the same degree of the zodiac. These occur roughly every 20 years and were the primary tool for astrological historians.</note></term>—is captured by this general phrase: the "Disturbance of things inherently unstable." 

<margin>Unrest.</margin>

Indeed, in those affairs and in those Republics <note>In the 17th-century sense, the "Republic" refers to the state or the "common wealth" in general, not necessarily a non-monarchical government.</note> which had already been undergoing gradual disturbance for a long time, new and significant upheavals were stirred up. I was able to safely predict three years ago that this would happen again this time.

<margin>But not the overthrow of Empires.</margin>

However, to establish a completely new Empire among men, to transfer Kingdoms and sovereign powers, or to manage changes in Religion—whether fostering those that improve or permitting those that decline for the worse—

<summary>The author argues that while "Great Conjunctions" and the "Fiery Trigon" coincide with periods of social and political unrest, they do not spontaneously create new empires or religions, but rather accelerate existing instabilities.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, Great Conjunction, Fiery Trigon, political unrest, history of religion, prophecy, Johannes Kepler</keywords>
